People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I think those notes about me are getting slipped or passed to the office… even though everybody keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just for a day, where you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I wish I could say there was, because I know that would probably sound better. People tell me they’re just notices on the doors, but it still doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, even if just for a brief instant, where you completely doubted that people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to doubt it, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself, “Maybe I’m making too much of it,” because I don’t want to seem unreasonable. But completely doubt it? No… not really. I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that those notes are about me getting passed to the office, even though everyone keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you for at least a week?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… not for a whole week. I wish I could say yes, because I know how it sounds, but it’s pretty much stayed with me. Even when I try to put it out of my mind, I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. Everybody says they’re just notices on the doors, and I hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think it could happen in real life. I know maybe it sounds like I’m making too much of regular apartment stuff, and I don’t want to come across that way. But people do watch who comes and goes, and offices do get notes or complaints. So when I see those notices on doors, it feels to me like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, even if everybody keeps saying that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to anyone, or is it something very unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mean, it’s not something that happens to everyone every day, no. But it doesn’t feel impossible to me. People in apartments notice who’s coming and going, and the office gets told things. I’m trying to say that right.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Everyone keeps saying they’re just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are watching you or talking about you for a specific reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s a “reason” exactly… I mean, I don’t want to sound like I’m accusing people for no reason. But it really feels like they’re watching when I leave and when I come back, and then somehow that gets passed along to the office. The notes on the doors—everyone says they’re just regular notices, but to me it feels like they’re about me, or connected to me somehow. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that people are watching you and passing notes about you, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to say it in a way that sounds bad, but yes, I do believe it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling to me. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and those notes they put on the doors… I think they’re getting passed to the office about me. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because things at my apartment have been bothering me. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m causing trouble or anything, but I’m pretty sure some of the people there are keeping track of when I come and go… and passing notes about me to the office. Everyone keeps saying they’re just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are watching you or talking about you for a specific reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s a “reason” exactly… I mean, I don’t want to sound like I’m accusing people for no reason. But it really feels like they’re watching when I leave and when I come back, and then somehow that gets passed along to the office. The notes on the doors—everyone says they’re just regular notices, but to me it feels like they’re about me, or connected to me somehow. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that people are watching you and passing notes about you, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to say it in a way that sounds bad, but yes, I do believe it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling to me. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and those notes they put on the doors… I think they’re getting passed to the office about me. I know people keep telling me they’re just regular notices, but that doesn’t seem right to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… before all this started, I didn’t really think that way, no. It’s been going on for, I guess, about a year and a month now. I know that sounds kind of specific, but that’s about how long I’ve been feeling like the neighbors and the office are involved like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about people watching you and passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess anything is possible, right? I don’t want to be stubborn or sound like I won’t listen. But honestly, Doctor, it doesn’t feel like I’m mistaken. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I think those notes about me are getting slipped or passed to the office… even though everybody keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just for a day, where you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I wish I could say there was, because I know that would probably sound better. But even on days when I try not to think about it, I still believe the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. People tell me they’re just notices on the doors, but it still doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, even if just for a brief instant, where you completely doubted that people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to doubt it, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself, “Maybe I’m making too much of it,” because I don’t want to seem unreasonable. But completely doubt it? No… not really. I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that those notes are about me getting passed to the office, even though everyone keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you for at least a week?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… not for a whole week. I wish I could say yes, because I know how it sounds, but it’s pretty much stayed with me. Even when I try to put it out of my mind, I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. Everybody says they’re just notices on the doors, and I hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think it could happen in real life. I know maybe it sounds like I’m making too much of regular apartment stuff, and I don’t want to come across that way. But people do watch who comes and goes, and offices do get notes or complaints. I’m trying to say that right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in a normal, everyday situation?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a normal apartment situation it could happen. It’s just that to me, it feels like those notes on the doors are really about me, like they’re being slipped to the office.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because things at my apartment have been bothering me. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m causing trouble or anything, but I’m pretty sure some of the people there are keeping track of when I come and go… and passing notes about me to the office. Everyone keeps saying they’re just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are watching you or talking about you for a specific reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s a “reason” exactly… I mean, I don’t want to sound like I’m accusing people for no reason. But it really feels like they’re watching when I leave and when I come back, and then somehow that gets passed along to the office. The notes on the doors—everyone says they’re just regular notices, but to me it feels like they’re about me, or connected to me somehow. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that people are watching you and passing notes about you, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to say it in a way that sounds bad, but yes, I do believe it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling to me. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and those notes they put on the doors… I think they’re getting passed to the office about me. I know people keep telling me they’re just regular notices, but that doesn’t seem right to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… before all this started, I didn’t really think that way, no. It’s been going on for, I guess, about a year and a month now. I know that sounds kind of specific, but that’s about how long I’ve been feeling like the neighbors and the office are involved like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about people watching you and passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess anything is possible, right? I don’t want to be stubborn or sound like I won’t listen. But honestly, Doctor, it doesn’t feel like I’m mistaken. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I think those notes about me are getting slipped or passed to the office… even though everybody keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just for a day, where you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I wish I could say there was, because I know that would probably sound better. But even on days when I try not to think about it, I still believe the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. People tell me they’re just notices on the doors, but it still doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, even if just for a brief instant, where you completely doubted that people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to doubt it, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself, “Maybe I’m making too much of it,” because I don’t want to seem unreasonable. But completely doubt it? No… not really. I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that those notes are about me getting passed to the office, even though everyone keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you for at least a week?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… not for a whole week. I wish I could say yes, because I know how it sounds, but it’s pretty much stayed with me. Even when I try to put it out of my mind, I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. Everybody says they’re just notices on the doors, and I hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think it could happen in real life. I know maybe it sounds like I’m making too much of regular apartment stuff, and I don’t want to come across that way. But people do watch who comes and goes, and offices do get notes or complaints. So when I see those notices on doors, it feels to me like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, even if everybody keeps saying that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to anyone, or is it something very unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mean, it’s not something that happens to everyone every day, no. But it doesn’t feel impossible to me. People in apartments notice who’s coming and going, and the office gets told things. So… maybe it’s unusual, but I don’t think it’s crazy to think they could be watching me and putting notes about me to the office. I’m trying to say that right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in a normal, everyday situation?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a normal apartment situation it could happen. I don’t mean to make it sound bigger than it is, but people do notice when you come and go, and the office does get papers or notices. It’s just that to me, it feels like those notes on the doors are really about me, like they’re being slipped to the office. I know everyone keeps telling me they’re just regular notices, and I’m trying to hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a month where you didn't think people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I wish I could say there was a long stretch where it wasn’t on my mind, but it’s been there for… about a year and a month now, I guess. Some days I try not to focus on it as much, but I don’t think I’ve gone a whole month without thinking they were watching or passing notes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in the real world, in a way that makes sense to most people?\n\nPatient: I think it could happen in the real world, yes… like, people at an apartment do watch who comes and goes, and the office can get notes or notices. I know most people might say, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” and I’m trying not to sound unreasonable. But to me it makes sense that they’re watching me and slipping notes about me to the office. Is that… is that answering it right?\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to someone else you know?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, I think it could happen to someone else too. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because things at my apartment have been bothering me. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m causing trouble or anything, but I’m pretty sure some of the people there are keeping track of when I come and go… and passing notes about me to the office. Everyone keeps saying they’re just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are watching you or talking about you for a specific reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s a “reason” exactly… I mean, I don’t want to sound like I’m accusing people for no reason. But it really feels like they’re watching when I leave and when I come back, and then somehow that gets passed along to the office. The notes on the doors—everyone says they’re just regular notices, but to me it feels like they’re about me, or connected to me somehow. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that people are watching you and passing notes about you, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to say it in a way that sounds bad, but yes, I do believe it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling to me. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and those notes they put on the doors… I think they’re getting passed to the office about me. I know people keep telling me they’re just regular notices, but that doesn’t seem right to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… before all this started, I didn’t really think that way, no. It’s been going on for, I guess, about a year and a month now. I know that sounds kind of specific, but that’s about how long I’ve been feeling like the neighbors and the office are involved like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about people watching you and passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess anything is possible, right? I don’t want to be stubborn or sound like I won’t listen. But honestly, Doctor, it doesn’t feel like I’m mistaken. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I think those notes about me are getting slipped or passed to the office… even though everybody keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just for a day, where you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I wish I could say there was, because I know that would probably sound better. But even on days when I try not to think about it, I still believe the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. People tell me they’re just notices on the doors, but it still doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, even if just for a brief instant, where you completely doubted that people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to doubt it, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself, “Maybe I’m making too much of it,” because I don’t want to seem unreasonable. But completely doubt it? No… not really. I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that those notes are about me getting passed to the office, even though everyone keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you for at least a week?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… not for a whole week. I wish I could say yes, because I know how it sounds, but it’s pretty much stayed with me. Even when I try to put it out of my mind, I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. Everybody says they’re just notices on the doors, and I hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think it could happen in real life. I know maybe it sounds like I’m making too much of regular apartment stuff, and I don’t want to come across that way. But people do watch who comes and goes, and offices do get notes or complaints. So when I see those notices on doors, it feels to me like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, even if everybody keeps saying that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to anyone, or is it something very unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mean, it’s not something that happens to everyone every day, no. But it doesn’t feel impossible to me. People in apartments notice who’s coming and going, and the office gets told things. So… maybe it’s unusual, but I don’t think it’s crazy to think they could be watching me and putting notes about me to the office. I’m trying to say that right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in a normal, everyday situation?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a normal apartment situation it could happen. It’s just that to me, it feels like those notes on the doors are really about me, like they’re being slipped to the office. I mean, I wish I could say there was a long stretch where it wasn’t on my mind, but it’s been there for… about a year and a month now, I guess. I know most people might say, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” and I’m trying not to sound unreasonable. But to me it makes sense that they’re watching me and slipping notes about me to the office. Even when I try to put it out of my mind, I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. Everybody says they’re just notices on the doors, and I hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think it could happen in real life. I know maybe it sounds like I’m making too much of regular apartment stuff, and I don’t want to come across that way. But people do watch who comes and goes, and offices do get notes or complaints. So when I see those notices on doors, it feels to me like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, even if everybody keeps saying that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to anyone, or is it something very unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mean, it’s not something that happens to everyone every day, no. But it doesn’t feel impossible to me. People in apartments notice who’s coming and going, and the office gets told things. So… maybe it’s unusual, but I don’t think it’s crazy to think they could be watching me and putting notes about me to the office. I’m trying to say that right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in a normal, everyday situation?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a normal apartment situation it could happen. I don’t mean to make it sound bigger than it is, but people do notice when you come and go, and the office does get papers or notices. It’s just that to me, it feels like those notes on the doors are really about me, like they’re being slipped to the office. I know everyone keeps telling me they’re just regular notices, and I’m trying to hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a month where you didn't think people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I wish I could say there was a long stretch where it wasn’t on my mind, but it’s been there for… about a year and a month now, I guess. Some days I try not to focus on it as much, but I don’t think I’ve gone a whole month without thinking they were watching or passing notes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in the real world, in a way that makes sense to most people?\n\nPatient: I think it could happen in the real world, yes… like, people at an apartment do watch who comes and goes, and the office can get notes or notices. I know most people might say, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” and I’m trying not to sound unreasonable. But to me it makes sense that they’re watching me and slipping notes about me to the office. Is that… is that answering it right?\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to someone else you know?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, I think it could happen to someone else too. If they lived in my apartment, people might notice when they come and go, and the office might get notes. I know everybody keeps saying the things on the doors are just notices, and maybe that’s how it looks to them… but with me, it really feels like they’re watching me and passing notes about me. I’m trying to explain it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be connected to people watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… mostly it’s that same thing, I think. The notes on the doors—everyone says they’re just regular notices, but to me it feels like they’re about me, or connected to me somehow. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that people are watching you and passing notes about you, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to say it in a way that sounds bad, but yes, I do believe it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling to me. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and those notes they put on the doors… I think they’re getting passed to the office about me. I know people keep telling me they’re just regular notices, but that doesn’t seem right to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… before all this started, I didn’t really think that way, no. It’s been going on for, I guess, about a year and a month now. I know that sounds kind of specific, but that’s about how long I’ve been feeling like the neighbors and the office are involved like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about people watching you and passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess anything is possible, right? I don’t want to be stubborn or sound like I won’t listen. But honestly, Doctor, it doesn’t feel like I’m mistaken. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I think those notes about me are getting slipped or passed to the office… even though everybody keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just for a day, where you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I wish I could say there was, because I know that would probably sound better. But even on days when I try not to think about it, I still believe the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. People tell me they’re just notices on the doors, but it still doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, even if just for a brief instant, where you completely doubted that people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to doubt it, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself, “Maybe I’m making too much of it,” because I don’t want to seem unreasonable. But completely doubt it? No… not really. I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that those notes are about me getting passed to the office, even though everyone keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you for at least a week?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… not for a whole week. I wish I could say yes, because I know how it sounds, but it’s pretty much stayed with me. Even when I try to put it out of my mind, I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. Everybody says they’re just notices on the doors, and I hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think it could happen in real life. I know maybe it sounds like I’m making too much of regular apartment stuff, and I don’t want to come across that way. But people do watch who comes and goes, and offices do get notes or complaints. So when I see those notices on doors, it feels to me like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, even if everybody keeps saying that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to anyone, or is it something very unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mean, it’s not something that happens to everyone every day, no. But it doesn’t feel impossible to me. People in apartments notice who’s coming and going, and the office gets told things. So… maybe it’s unusual, but I don’t think it’s crazy to think they could be watching me and putting notes about me to the office. I’m trying to say that right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in a normal, everyday situation?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a normal apartment situation it could happen. I don’t mean to make it sound bigger than it is, but people do notice when you come and go, and the office does get papers or notices. It’s just that to me, it feels like those notes on the doors are really about me, like they’re being slipped to the office. I know everyone keeps telling me they’re just regular notices, and I’m trying to hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a month where you didn't think people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I wish I could say there was a long stretch where it wasn’t on my mind, but it’s been there for… about a year and a month now, I guess. Some days I try not to focus on it as much, but I don’t think I’ve gone a whole month without thinking they were watching or passing notes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in the real world, in a way that makes sense to most people?\n\nPatient: I think it could happen in the real world, yes… like, people at an apartment do watch who comes and goes, and the office can get notes or notices. I know most people might say, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” and I’m trying not to sound unreasonable. But to me it makes sense that they’re watching me and slipping notes about me to the office. Is that… is that answering it right?\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to someone else you know?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, I think it could happen to someone else too. If they lived in my apartment, people might notice when they come and go, and the office might get notes. I know everybody keeps saying the things on the doors are just notices, and maybe that’s how it looks to them… but with me, it really feels like they’re watching me and passing notes about me. I’m trying to explain it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be connected to people watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… mostly it’s that same thing, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s why I came in—because I really do feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and then somehow notes are getting to the office about me.\n\nEverybody keeps telling me those papers are just regular notices on the doors, and I’m trying to take that in, but… it doesn’t feel that way to me. It feels connected to me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you hold to be true, even if others might not agree with them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything else… not really. It’s mainly that I know the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and I feel like they’re slipping notes about me to the office.\n\nI know other people keep saying they’re just notices on the doors, and I’m trying to hear that, I am. But it still feels true to me that it’s about me. I hope that’s okay to say.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But even on days when I try not to think about it, I still believe the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. People tell me they’re just notices on the doors, but it still doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, even if just for a brief instant, where you completely doubted that people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to doubt it, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself, “Maybe I’m making too much of it,” because I don’t want to seem unreasonable. But completely doubt it? No… not really. I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that those notes are about me getting passed to the office, even though everyone keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you for at least a week?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… not for a whole week. I wish I could say yes, because I know how it sounds, but it’s pretty much stayed with me. Even when I try to put it out of my mind, I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. Everybody says they’re just notices on the doors, and I hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think it could happen in real life. I know maybe it sounds like I’m making too much of regular apartment stuff, and I don’t want to come across that way. But people do watch who comes and goes, and offices do get notes or complaints. So when I see those notices on doors, it feels to me like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, even if everybody keeps saying that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to anyone, or is it something very unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mean, it’s not something that happens to everyone every day, no. But it doesn’t feel impossible to me. People in apartments notice who’s coming and going, and the office gets told things. So… maybe it’s unusual, but I don’t think it’s crazy to think they could be watching me and putting notes about me to the office. I’m trying to say that right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in a normal, everyday situation?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a normal apartment situation it could happen. I don’t mean to make it sound bigger than it is, but people do notice when you come and go, and the office does get papers or notices. It’s just that to me, it feels like those notes on the doors are really about me, like they’re being slipped to the office. I know everyone keeps telling me they’re just regular notices, and I’m trying to hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a month where you didn't think people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I wish I could say there was a long stretch where it wasn’t on my mind, but it’s been there for… about a year and a month now, I guess. Some days I try not to focus on it as much, but I don’t think I’ve gone a whole month without thinking they were watching or passing notes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in the real world, in a way that makes sense to most people?\n\nPatient: I think it could happen in the real world, yes… like, people at an apartment do watch who comes and goes, and the office can get notes or notices. I know most people might say, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” and I’m trying not to sound unreasonable. But to me it makes sense that they’re watching me and slipping notes about me to the office. Is that… is that answering it right?\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to someone else you know?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, I think it could happen to someone else too. If they lived in my apartment, people might notice when they come and go, and the office might get notes. I know everybody keeps saying the things on the doors are just notices, and maybe that’s how it looks to them… but with me, it really feels like they’re watching me and passing notes about me. I’m trying to explain it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be connected to people watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… mostly it’s that same thing, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s why I came in—because I really do feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and then somehow notes are getting to the office about me.\n\nEverybody keeps telling me those papers are just regular notices on the doors, and I’m trying to take that in, but… it doesn’t feel that way to me. It feels connected to me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you hold to be true, even if others might not agree with them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything else… not really. It’s mainly that I know the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and I feel like they’re slipping notes about me to the office.\n\nI know other people keep saying they’re just notices on the doors, and I’m trying to hear that, I am. But it still feels true to me that it’s about me. I hope that’s okay to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that your belief about being watched and notes being passed is not true?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mean, I’m trying to leave room for that, because everybody keeps telling me it’s just notices on the doors and maybe I’m reading it wrong.\n\nBut honestly, doctor, it really feels true to me. That’s why I came in. I feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting to the office. I don’t want to sound unreasonable, but it’s hard for me to believe it’s not happening.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you as a strange or unusual belief to hold?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe it does sound unusual when you say it that way. I don’t want to sound odd or like I’m making trouble.\n\nBut to me it feels real. That’s why I came in, because I feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that they’re slipping notes about me to the office. I know everybody says they’re just notices on the doors, and I’m trying to accept that possibility… but it still feels like it’s about me. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your belief that people are watching you and passing notes about you could be proven or disproven?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 59 / not_in_workforce / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings and slipping notes about me to the office, even though everybody keeps telling me it's just notices on the doors.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think… maybe it could be disproven if someone could show me those notes and they really are just regular notices, or if the office said there’s nothing about me. I’m trying to be fair about it.

But the way it feels to me, I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go and slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody keeps telling me it’s just notices on the doors, and I don’t want to argue, but it still feels like it’s about me. Does that make sense?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because things at my apartment have been bothering me. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m causing trouble or anything, but I’m pretty sure some of the people there are keeping track of when I come and go… and passing notes about me to the office. Everyone keeps saying they’re just regular notices on the doors, but it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are watching you or talking about you for a specific reason?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s a “reason” exactly… I mean, I don’t want to sound like I’m accusing people for no reason. But it really feels like they’re watching when I leave and when I come back, and then somehow that gets passed along to the office. The notes on the doors—everyone says they’re just regular notices, but to me it feels like they’re about me, or connected to me somehow. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you really believe that people are watching you and passing notes about you, or is it just a feeling you have?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to say it in a way that sounds bad, but yes, I do believe it’s happening. It’s not just a feeling to me. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and those notes they put on the doors… I think they’re getting passed to the office about me. I know people keep telling me they’re just regular notices, but that doesn’t seem right to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… before all this started, I didn’t really think that way, no. It’s been going on for, I guess, about a year and a month now. I know that sounds kind of specific, but that’s about how long I’ve been feeling like the neighbors and the office are involved like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be mistaken about people watching you and passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess anything is possible, right? I don’t want to be stubborn or sound like I won’t listen. But honestly, Doctor, it doesn’t feel like I’m mistaken. I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I leave and come back, and I think those notes about me are getting slipped or passed to the office… even though everybody keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just for a day, where you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I wish I could say there was, because I know that would probably sound better. But even on days when I try not to think about it, I still believe the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. People tell me they’re just notices on the doors, but it still doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, even if just for a brief instant, where you completely doubted that people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to doubt it, if that makes sense. Like I’ll tell myself, “Maybe I’m making too much of it,” because I don’t want to seem unreasonable. But completely doubt it? No… not really. I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that those notes are about me getting passed to the office, even though everyone keeps saying they’re just notices on the doors.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you didn't believe people were watching you or passing notes about you for at least a week?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… not for a whole week. I wish I could say yes, because I know how it sounds, but it’s pretty much stayed with me. Even when I try to put it out of my mind, I still feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting slipped to the office. Everybody says they’re just notices on the doors, and I hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in real life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think it could happen in real life. I know maybe it sounds like I’m making too much of regular apartment stuff, and I don’t want to come across that way. But people do watch who comes and goes, and offices do get notes or complaints. So when I see those notices on doors, it feels to me like they’re slipping notes about me to the office, even if everybody keeps saying that’s not what it is.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to anyone, or is it something very unusual?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mean, it’s not something that happens to everyone every day, no. But it doesn’t feel impossible to me. People in apartments notice who’s coming and going, and the office gets told things. So… maybe it’s unusual, but I don’t think it’s crazy to think they could be watching me and putting notes about me to the office. I’m trying to say that right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in a normal, everyday situation?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a normal apartment situation it could happen. I don’t mean to make it sound bigger than it is, but people do notice when you come and go, and the office does get papers or notices. It’s just that to me, it feels like those notes on the doors are really about me, like they’re being slipped to the office. I know everyone keeps telling me they’re just regular notices, and I’m trying to hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a month where you didn't think people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I wish I could say there was a long stretch where it wasn’t on my mind, but it’s been there for… about a year and a month now, I guess. Some days I try not to focus on it as much, but I don’t think I’ve gone a whole month without thinking they were watching or passing notes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in the real world, in a way that makes sense to most people?\n\nPatient: I think it could happen in the real world, yes… like, people at an apartment do watch who comes and goes, and the office can get notes or notices. I know most people might say, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” and I’m trying not to sound unreasonable. But to me it makes sense that they’re watching me and slipping notes about me to the office. Is that… is that answering it right?\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to someone else you know?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, I think it could happen to someone else too. If they lived in my apartment, people might notice when they come and go, and the office might get notes. I know everybody keeps saying the things on the doors are just notices, and maybe that’s how it looks to them… but with me, it really feels like they’re watching me and passing notes about me. I’m trying to explain it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be connected to people watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… mostly it’s that same thing, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s why I came in—because I really do feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and then somehow notes are getting to the office about me.\n\nEverybody keeps telling me those papers are just regular notices on the doors, and I’m trying to take that in, but… it doesn’t feel that way to me. It feels connected to me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you hold to be true, even if others might not agree with them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything else… not really. It’s mainly that I know the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and I feel like they’re slipping notes about me to the office.\n\nI know other people keep saying they’re just notices on the doors, and I’m trying to hear that, I am. But it still feels true to me that it’s about me. I hope that’s okay to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that your belief about being watched and notes being passed is not true?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mean, I’m trying to leave room for that, because everybody keeps telling me it’s just notices on the doors and maybe I’m reading it wrong.\n\nBut honestly, doctor, it really feels true to me. That’s why I came in. I feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting to the office. I don’t want to sound unreasonable, but it’s hard for me to believe it’s not happening.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you as a strange or unusual belief to hold?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe it does sound unusual when you say it that way. I don’t want to sound odd or like I’m making trouble.\n\nBut to me it feels real. That’s why I came in, because I feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that they’re slipping notes about me to the office. I know everybody says they’re just notices on the doors, and I’m trying to accept that possibility… but it still feels like it’s about me. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that people are watching you and passing notes about you could be proven or disproven?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe it could be disproven if someone could show me those notes and they really are just regular notices, or if the office said there’s nothing about me. So… maybe it’s unusual, but I don’t think it’s crazy to think they could be watching me and putting notes about me to the office. I’m trying to say that right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in a normal, everyday situation?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a normal apartment situation it could happen. I don’t mean to make it sound bigger than it is, but people do notice when you come and go, and the office does get papers or notices. It’s just that to me, it feels like those notes on the doors are really about me, like they’re being slipped to the office. I know everyone keeps telling me they’re just regular notices, and I’m trying to hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a month where you didn't think people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I wish I could say there was a long stretch where it wasn’t on my mind, but it’s been there for… about a year and a month now, I guess. Some days I try not to focus on it as much, but I don’t think I’ve gone a whole month without thinking they were watching or passing notes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in the real world, in a way that makes sense to most people?\n\nPatient: I think it could happen in the real world, yes… like, people at an apartment do watch who comes and goes, and the office can get notes or notices. I know most people might say, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” and I’m trying not to sound unreasonable. But to me it makes sense that they’re watching me and slipping notes about me to the office. Is that… is that answering it right?\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to someone else you know?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, I think it could happen to someone else too. If they lived in my apartment, people might notice when they come and go, and the office might get notes. I know everybody keeps saying the things on the doors are just notices, and maybe that’s how it looks to them… but with me, it really feels like they’re watching me and passing notes about me. I’m trying to explain it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be connected to people watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… mostly it’s that same thing, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s why I came in—because I really do feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and then somehow notes are getting to the office about me.\n\nEverybody keeps telling me those papers are just regular notices on the doors, and I’m trying to take that in, but… it doesn’t feel that way to me. It feels connected to me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you hold to be true, even if others might not agree with them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything else… not really. It’s mainly that I know the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and I feel like they’re slipping notes about me to the office.\n\nI know other people keep saying they’re just notices on the doors, and I’m trying to hear that, I am. But it still feels true to me that it’s about me. I hope that’s okay to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that your belief about being watched and notes being passed is not true?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mean, I’m trying to leave room for that, because everybody keeps telling me it’s just notices on the doors and maybe I’m reading it wrong.\n\nBut honestly, doctor, it really feels true to me. I’m trying to say that right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in a normal, everyday situation?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a normal apartment situation it could happen. I don’t mean to make it sound bigger than it is, but people do notice when you come and go, and the office does get papers or notices. It’s just that to me, it feels like those notes on the doors are really about me, like they’re being slipped to the office. I know everyone keeps telling me they’re just regular notices, and I’m trying to hear that… but I don’t really believe that’s all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a month where you didn't think people were watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I wish I could say there was a long stretch where it wasn’t on my mind, but it’s been there for… about a year and a month now, I guess. Some days I try not to focus on it as much, but I don’t think I’ve gone a whole month without thinking they were watching or passing notes.\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen in the real world, in a way that makes sense to most people?\n\nPatient: I think it could happen in the real world, yes… like, people at an apartment do watch who comes and goes, and the office can get notes or notices. I know most people might say, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” and I’m trying not to sound unreasonable. But to me it makes sense that they’re watching me and slipping notes about me to the office. Is that… is that answering it right?\n\nDoctor: Would you say the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you is something that could happen to someone else you know?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, I think it could happen to someone else too. If they lived in my apartment, people might notice when they come and go, and the office might get notes. I know everybody keeps saying the things on the doors are just notices, and maybe that’s how it looks to them… but with me, it really feels like they’re watching me and passing notes about me. I’m trying to explain it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think might be connected to people watching you or passing notes about you?\n\nPatient: Um… mostly it’s that same thing, I think. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s why I came in—because I really do feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and then somehow notes are getting to the office about me.\n\nEverybody keeps telling me those papers are just regular notices on the doors, and I’m trying to take that in, but… it doesn’t feel that way to me. It feels connected to me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you hold to be true, even if others might not agree with them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s anything else… not really. It’s mainly that I know the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and I feel like they’re slipping notes about me to the office.\n\nI know other people keep saying they’re just notices on the doors, and I’m trying to hear that, I am. But it still feels true to me that it’s about me. I hope that’s okay to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that your belief about being watched and notes being passed is not true?\n\nPatient: I guess… I mean, I’m trying to leave room for that, because everybody keeps telling me it’s just notices on the doors and maybe I’m reading it wrong.\n\nBut honestly, doctor, it really feels true to me. That’s why I came in. I feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that notes about me are getting to the office. I don’t want to sound unreasonable, but it’s hard for me to believe it’s not happening.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that your neighbors and the office are watching you and passing notes about you as a strange or unusual belief to hold?\n\nPatient: I guess… yeah, maybe it does sound unusual when you say it that way. I don’t want to sound odd or like I’m making trouble.\n\nBut to me it feels real. That’s why I came in, because I feel like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and that they’re slipping notes about me to the office. I know everybody says they’re just notices on the doors, and I’m trying to accept that possibility… but it still feels like it’s about me. Is that an okay way to explain it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that people are watching you and passing notes about you could be proven or disproven?\n\nPatient: I think… maybe it could be disproven if someone could show me those notes and they really are just regular notices, or if the office said there’s nothing about me. I’m trying to be fair about it.\n\nBut the way it feels to me, I came in because I know the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go and slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody keeps telling me it’s just notices on the doors, and I don’t want to argue, but it still feels like it’s about me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief that people are watching you and passing notes about you would change if everyone around you, including the office, told you it wasn't true?\n\nPatient: I’d like to say yes, doctor, because I don’t want to be stubborn about it. If the office and everybody told me there’s nothing going on, I’d try to take that in.\n\nBut… I don’t know if it would really change right away. It still feels to me like the people at my apartment are watching when I come and go, and like notes about me are getting slipped to the office. Even when people say it’s just notices on doors, part of me still feels like it’s about me. Is that… is that the kind of answer you mean?"
